On the B-series composition theorem
John C. Butcher, Taketomo Mitsui, Yuto Miyatake, Shun Sato

Abstract
The B-series composition theorem has been an important topic in numerical analysis of ordinary differential equations for the past-half century. Traditional proofs of this theorem rely on labelled trees, whereas recent developments in B-series analysis favour the use of unlabelled trees. In this paper, we present a new proof of the B-series composition theorem that does not depend on labelled trees. A key challenge in this approach is accurately counting combinations related to “pruning.” This challenge is overcome by introducing the concept of “assignment.”



keywords
B-series, Composition, Rooted trees, Pruning

1
Introduction

Given an
n
-dimensional initial value problem

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}y(x)=f(y(x)),\quad y(x_{0})=y_{0},

where
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
B-series play an indispensable role
in the analysis of numerical methods, such as Runge–Kutta methods.
The importance of B-series was recognised in the early 1970s and led to major developments in algebraic analysis.
The composition rule was initially proposed by Butcher in
[
1
]
, and a more direct proof was provided by Hairer and Wanner
[
4
]
(see also Theorem II.12.6 in
[
6
]
).
A proof with the more modern notation is also given in
[
5
]
.
These proofs make extensive use of labelled trees.

A recent monograph by the first author in 2021
[
2
]
attempted to establish the composition theorem without the use of labelled trees.
After extensive discussion while translating the book into Japanese, a new proof was found based on a correction to the proof in
[
2
]
.
This translation has been published
[
3
]
, though the new proof has remained confined to the Japanese language until now.
This paper seeks to present the new proof in English.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}\lxSVG@fill\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 29.53 M 2.77 29.53 C 2.77 31.06 1.53 32.29 0 32.29 C -1.53 32.29 -2.77 31.06 -2.77 29.53 C -2.77 28 -1.53 26.76 0 26.76 C 1.53 26.76 2.77 28 2.77 29.53 Z M 0 29.53}{stroke:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},\, \ldots}\bigg\}

(1)

be the set of rooted trees and
\text{\sf B}^{*}
as the set of all mappings from
\varnothing\cup\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}
to
\mathbb{R}
.
For
a\in\text{\sf B}^{*}
,
the B-series
({\bf B}_{h}y_{0})a
is a formal series defined by

({\bf B}_{h}y_{0})a=a(\varnothing)y_{0}+\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}}\frac{h^{|t|}a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}),

(2)

where the elementary differential
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})
is implicitly evaluated at
y_{0}
.
In particular, when
a(\varnothing)=1
, this formal series not only encompasses the Taylor series expansion for the exact solution
y(x_{0}+h)
but also includes approximate solutions obtained by applying important classes of numerical methods, such as Runge–Kutta methods.
By using the affine subspace
\text{\sf B}=\{a\in\text{\sf B}^{*}\mid a(\varnothing)=1\}
, the B-series composition theorem, which is the focus of this paper, can be summarized as follows.

Theorem 1
.

Let
a\in\text{\sf B}
and
b\in\text{\sf B}^{*}
. Then,

\big({\bf B}_{h}({\bf B}_{h}y_{0})a\big)b

is also a B-series
({\bf B}_{h}y_{0})(ab)
, and the map
ab\in\text{\sf B}^{*}
is given as

\displaystyle(ab)(\varnothing)

\displaystyle=b(\varnothing),

\displaystyle(ab)(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})

\displaystyle=b(\varnothing)a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})+\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}\leq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}}b(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}),\quad\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}.

(3)

Once the following lemma has been proved,
the proof of Theorem
1
is straightforward.

Lemma 2
.

Let
a\in\text{\sf B}
.
For
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}
, it follows that

\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}|}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})\big(({\bf B}_{h}a)y_{0}\big)=\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\geq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})}a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})(y_{0}).

(4)

The main contribution of the paper is to provide a new proof for Lemma
2
.
The major challenge in avoiding labelled trees arises from the complexity of counting trees.
To clarify this issue, we introduce the concept of an
assignment
of two trees, which plays a pivotal role in the counting process.

1.2
Notation

A graph
G=(V,E)
is a pair of the sets
V
and
E
, where
V
is the finite set of vertices and
E\subseteq V\times V
is the set of edges.
A (rooted) tree
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=(V,E,r)
is a triplet such that
(V,E)
is a connected graph with at least one vertex and with no loops, and
r\in V
.
We identify two trees
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=(V,E,r)
and
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}=(V^{\prime},E^{\prime},r^{\prime})
if there exists a bijective map
\pi\colon V\to V^{\prime}
such that
(v_{1},v_{2})\in E\iff(\pi(v_{1}),\pi(v_{2}))\in E^{\prime}
.
Recalling the introduction of trees given by (
1
), we denote the order of
t
, that is the number of vertices, as
|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|
.
The unique tree with order
1
is denoted by
\tau
.
A new tree obtained by connecting the roots of
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2},\dots,\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}

with a new root
is denoted by

\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2}\dots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}].

If some of
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2},\dots,\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}
are equal to each other, we write

\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}^{k_{1}}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2}^{k_{2}}\dots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}^{k_{m}}],

for example
\Big[\hbox to4pt{\vbox to11.11pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=1.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}\lxSVG@fill\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.91 9.84 M 8.67 9.84 C 8.67 11.37 7.43 12.61 5.91 12.61 C 4.38 12.61 3.14 11.37 3.14 9.84 C 3.14 8.31 4.38 7.08 5.91 7.08 C 7.43 7.08 8.67 8.31 8.67 9.84 Z M 5.91 9.84}{stroke:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,}^{2}\Big]
.
We introduce
a\text{\rm D}:\varnothing\cup\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}\to\mathbb{R}
by generalizing
a\in\text{\sf B}
:

\displaystyle(a\text{\rm D})(\varnothing)=0,\quad(a\text{\rm D})(\tau)=1,

\displaystyle(a\text{\rm D})(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{i}),\quad\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1},\dots,\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}].

The function
\sigma:\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}\to\mathbb{R}
, called the symmetry, is defined recursively by

\displaystyle\sigma(\tau)

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{m}k_{i}!\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{i})^{k_{i}},\qquad\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}^{k_{1}}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}^{k_{m}}].

(5)

The elementary differential associated with a tree
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2}\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{n}]
is defined by

\displaystyle\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf f\hskip 0.4pt},

(6)

\displaystyle\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})

\displaystyle=\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf f\hskip 0.4pt}^{(n)}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1})\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2})\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{n}),\quad\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1},\dots,\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}].

(7)

Here, bold notation indicates the omission of
(y_{0})
, i.e.
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf f\hskip 0.4pt}=f(y_{0})
and
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})=\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})(y_{0})
.
If another vector is taken as an argument, it will be written explicitly.
Additionally,
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf f\hskip 0.4pt}^{(n)}
denotes the Fréchet derivative as a multi-linear operator evaluated at
y_{0}
.
For later use, we slightly generalize the definition of elementary differentials.
For the tree
t
,
we introduce the concept of a “stump”
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\ast^{n}
, which is a tree-like object with
n
edges connected to the root
with the other ends of these edges left blank.
We regard the stump
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\ast^{n}
as a mapping
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\ast^{n}\colon\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}^{n}\to\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}
satisfying

\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\ast^{n}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{1}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{2}\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{n}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2}\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{1}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{2}\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{n}],

where
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2}\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}]
(see
[
2
]
for details on the stumps).
For a tree
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2}\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}]
, we define

\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\ast^{n})=\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf f\hskip 0.4pt}^{(m+n)}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1})\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2})\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}).

A forest is a juxtaposition of trees.
The set of forests
F
can be defined
as the commutative semigroup with identity
1
, generated by
T
so that it satisfies the following relations:

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\in\text{\rm F},

\displaystyle\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\;\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}=\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}\;\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\

\displaystyle\in\text{\rm F},\qquad\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt},\quad\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\rm F}.

We denote by
|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}|

the order of
f
equal to
the total of the orders of the trees comprising
f
, so that
|[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]|=1+|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}|
.
The forest space
F
is a space of formal linear combination of forests, i.e. an element
F
of
F
can be written as
\text{\scriptsize\sf F}=\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\rm F}}c(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt})\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}
, where
c\colon\text{\rm F}\to\mathbb{R}
.
The forest space
F
is a unitary commutative ring with the obvious addition and multiplication.
Then, for
a\in\text{\sf B}
, there exists a unique homomorphic extension to the forest space, which is again denoted by
a\colon\text{\small\sf F}\to\mathbb{R}
.

A tree
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}=(V^{\prime},E^{\prime},r^{\prime})
is called a subtree of another tree
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=(V,E,r)
if there exists an injective mapping
\pi\colon V^{\prime}\to V
such that
\pi(r^{\prime})=r
and
(\pi(v^{\prime}_{1}),\pi(v^{\prime}_{2}))\in E
for all
(v^{\prime}_{1},v^{\prime}_{2})\in E^{\prime}
.
We denote
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}
being a subtree of
t
as
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}\leq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}
or
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\geq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}
.

For the pair of trees
(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})
such that
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\geq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}
, the pruning
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}
is defined as a formal sum of the possible ways in which
t
can be pruned to form
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}
.
To define the pruning formally, we introduce the set

\Pi(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})\coloneqq\{\pi\colon V^{\prime}\to V\mid\pi\text{ is injective, }\pi(r^{\prime})=r,\ (v^{\prime}_{1},v^{\prime}_{2})\in E^{\prime}\Rightarrow(\pi(v^{\prime}_{1}),\pi(v^{\prime}_{2}))\in E\}.

Then, the pruning is given by

\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{\pi\in\Pi(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})}G(V\setminus\pi(V^{\prime})),

where
G(V\setminus\pi(V^{\prime}))
denotes the induced subgraph of the vertex set
V\setminus\pi(V^{\prime})
,
i.e.
G(V\setminus\pi(V^{\prime}))
is the graph with the vertex set
V\setminus\pi(V^{\prime})
and the edge set
\{(v_{1},v_{2})\mid v_{1},v_{2}\in V\setminus\pi(V^{\prime}),\ (v_{1},v_{2})\in E\}
.

Proposition
3
and the proof of Lemma
2

The following proposition is a special case
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}=\tau
in Lemma
2
.
We show its proof for the readers’ convenience as it is not
given in
[
2
]
.

Proposition 3

(cf.
(
2
, Corollary 3.4F)
)
.

Let
a\in\text{\sf B}
.
Then, for any positive integer
n
, it holds that

f^{(n)}\left(({\bf B}_{h}a)y_{0}\right)=\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}}h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|-1}\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})^{-1}(a\text{\rm D})(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\ast^{n})=\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\rm F}}h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}|}\sigma([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}])^{-1}(a\text{\rm D})([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}])\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\ast^{n}).

Proof.

By introducing
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf K\hskip 0.4pt}=\{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf k\hskip 0.4pt}=[k_{1},k_{2},\dots,k_{m}]\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{m}\mid m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\}
, Taylor’s theorem implies that

f^{(n)}\bigg(y_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\delta_{i}\bigg)=\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf k\hskip 0.4pt}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf K\hskip 0.4pt}}\frac{1}{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf k\hskip 0.4pt}!}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf f\hskip 0.4pt}^{(n+|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf k\hskip 0.4pt}|)}\delta_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots\delta_{m}^{k_{m}},

where
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf k\hskip 0.4pt}!\coloneqq\prod_{i=1}^{m}k_{i}!
and
|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf k\hskip 0.4pt}|=\sum_{i=1}^{m}k_{i}
.

By setting
\delta=({\bf B}_{h}a)y_{0}-y_{0}
in the above equation, we have

\displaystyle f^{(n)}\left(({\bf B}_{h}a)y_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf k\hskip 0.4pt}}\frac{1}{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf k\hskip 0.4pt}!}\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}}\cdots\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}}\frac{h^{\sum k_{i}|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{i}|}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1})^{k_{1}}\cdots\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m})^{k_{m}}}a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1})^{k_{1}}\cdots a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m})^{k_{m}}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf f\hskip 0.4pt}^{(n+|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf k\hskip 0.4pt}|)}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1})^{k_{1}}\cdots\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{m})^{k_{m}}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|-1}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})}a\text{\rm D}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\ast^{n}).

This establishes the first equality in the proposition.
The second equality is straightforward since
[\cdot]\colon\text{\rm F}\to\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm T\hskip 0.3pt}
is bijective.
∎

We now give a new proof for Lemma
2
.

Proof.

The case
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}=\tau
is covered by Proposition
3
. The general case will be proved by induction with respect to the operator
[\cdot]
.
Below, let
y_{1}=\left({\bf B}_{h}a\right)y_{0}
.

Consider the tree
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}=\left[\prod^{n^{\prime}}_{i=1}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{k^{\prime}_{i}}\right]
, where
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{1},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{2},\dots,\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}}
are distinct.
Let
N^{\prime}=\sum_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}k^{\prime}_{i}
.
Using Proposition
3
and the induction hypothesis, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}|}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})\left(y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}|}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})}f^{(N^{\prime})}(y_{1})\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)\left(y_{1}\right)\right)^{k^{\prime}_{i}}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}|}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})}\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\rm F}}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\sigma\left([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\right)}a\text{\rm D}\left([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\right)\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}\left([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\ast^{N^{\prime}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)\left(y_{1}\right)\right)^{k^{\prime}_{i}}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}|}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})}\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\rm F}}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\sigma\left([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\right)}a\text{\rm D}\left([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\right)\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}\left([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\ast^{N^{\prime}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{k^{\prime}_{i}}\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\geq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}|}\sigma\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)}{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}|}\sigma\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\right)}a\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\right).

Now, define the set

S(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})=\left\{\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt},\{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\}\right):\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}\in\text{\rm F},\ \text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\geq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\ (i=1,2,\dots,n^{\prime};\,j=1,2,\dots,k^{\prime}_{i})\right\}

and let
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=\left[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}\prod_{i,j}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\right]
.
Then, we have

\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}|}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})\left(y_{1}\right)=\sum_{\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt},\{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\}\right)\in S(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}k^{\prime}_{i}!\right)\sigma\left([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\right)\prod_{i,j}\sigma\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\right)}a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt})\prod_{i,j}a\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\right).

(8)

Here, we have used the relation
\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})=\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}(k_{i}^{\prime}!)(\sigma(k_{i}^{\prime}))^{k_{i}^{\prime}}
.

Next, we consider the right-hand side of Lemma
2
.
To deal with the pruning
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}
, where
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=\left[\prod^{n}_{j=1}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\right)^{k_{j}}\right]
and
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}=\left[\prod^{n^{\prime}}_{i=1}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{k^{\prime}_{i}}\right]

with distinct trees
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2},\dots,\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{n}
and
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{1},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{2},\dots,\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}}
(note that
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{i}
can be equal to
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{j}
),
we introduce the following definition.
An
(n^{\prime}+1)\times n
non-negative integer matrix
m
(the
(i,j)
entry of
m
is denoted by
m_{ij}
, where
i=0,1,...,n^{\prime}
and
j=1,2,...,n
)
is said to be an assignment for the pair
(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})
if it satisfies the following three conditions:

[(iii)]

(i)

if
m_{ij}>0
, then
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\geq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}
,

(ii)

for all
j
,
\sum_{i=0}^{n’}m_{ij}=k_{j}
, and

(iii)

for all
i\neq 0
,
\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_{ij}=k’_{i}
.

Each assignment
m
corresponds to a specific pruning operation given by

\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}^{m_{0j}}\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{m_{ij}}\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\prod_{i=0}^{n^{\prime}}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{m_{ij}},

where
t^{\prime}_{0}=\varnothing
and we have adopted the convention
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\setminus\varnothing=\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}
.
Let
M(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})
be the set of all assignments for
(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})
.
Then, we have

\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}=\sum_{m\in M(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{k_{j}!}{\prod_{i=0}^{n^{\prime}}m_{ij}!}\prod_{i=0}^{n^{\prime}}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{m_{ij}}\right).

Here, the multinomial coefficient represents the number of combinations
to assign the trees
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}^{k_{j}}
to the trees
\{\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{m_{ij}}\}_{i=0}^{n^{\prime}}

(see Remark
1
for an illustrating example of the relation between assignments and pruning).
Using this expression of pruning and letting
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}^{m_{0j}}
, the summed expression of the right-hand side of Lemma
2
can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\sigma\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\right)}a\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}\right)\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!\left(\sigma\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\right)\right)^{k_{j}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{m\in M(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{k_{j}!}{\prod_{i=0}^{n^{\prime}}m_{ij}!}a\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}^{m_{0j}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}a\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{m_{ij}}\right)\cdot\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{m\in M(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\sigma([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}])\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(m_{ij}!\left(\sigma\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\right)\right)^{m_{ij}}\right)}\cdot a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt})\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}a\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{m_{ij}}\cdot\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}).

On the other hand, in the summation in (
8
), the term which corresponds to an assignment
m
appears
\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\frac{k^{\prime}_{i}!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}m_{ij}!}
times.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}|}}{\sigma(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})\left(y_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt},\{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\}\right)\in S(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime})}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}k^{\prime}_{i}!\right)\sigma\left([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\right)\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\sigma\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\right)}a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt})\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}a\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{ij}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\geq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}}\sum_{m\in M(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}k^{\prime}_{i}!}{\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}m_{ij}!}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}k^{\prime}_{i}!\right)\sigma\left([\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt}]\right)\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\sigma\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\right)\right)^{m_{ij}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot a(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm f\hskip 0.3pt})\prod_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}a\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{j}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{m_{ij}}\cdot\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\geq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}}\frac{h^{|\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}|}}{\sigma\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\right)}a\left(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}\setminus\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}\right)\text{\hskip 0.3pt\bf F\hskip 0.4pt}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}),

which proves the lemma.
∎

Remark 1
.

In this remark, we explain how the concept of assignment can be used to consider pruning, using the following example:

t

In this case, these trees can be expressed as
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}=[\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}^{k_{1}}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{2}^{k_{2}}\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{3}^{k_{3}}]
and
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}=[(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{1})^{k^{\prime}_{1}}(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{2})^{k^{\prime}_{2}}]
, where

\displaystyle\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}

\displaystyle=\hbox to4pt{\vbox to4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=1.0pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{

\displaystyle k_{1}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle k_{2}

\displaystyle=3,

\displaystyle k_{3}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle k^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle k^{\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle=2.

Since
\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}_{1}\geq\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime}_{2}
does not hold, the condition (i) of assignments implies that
m_{21}=0
.
Consequently, it is easy to verify

M(\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt}^{\prime},\text{\hskip 0.3pt\rm t\hskip 0.3pt})=\left\{\begin{bmatrix}0&1&1\\
1&0&0\\
0&2&0\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}0&2&0\\
1&0&0\\
0&1&1\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}1&0&1\\
0&1&0\\
0&2&0\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}1&1&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&1&1\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}1&1&0\\
0&0&1\\
0&2&0\end{bmatrix}\right\}.
